=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given positive integers N, M, K, and a sequence of non-negative integers: A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N).
For a non-empty non-negative integer sequence B=(B_1,B_2,\ldots,B_{|B|}), we define its score as follows.

- If the length of B is a multiple of M: (B_1 \oplus B_2 \oplus \dots \oplus B_{|B|})^K
- Otherwise: 0

Here, \oplus represents the bitwise XOR.
Find the sum, modulo 998244353, of the scores of the 2^N-1 non-empty subsequences of A.
What is bitwise XOR? The bitwise XOR of non-negative integers A and B, denoted as A \oplus B, is defined as follows: - In the binary representation of A \oplus B, the digit at position 2^k (k \geq 0) is 1 if exactly one of A and B has a 1 in that position in their binary representations, and 0 otherwise. For example, 3 \oplus 5 = 6 (in binary: 011 \oplus 101 = 110). In general, the XOR of k integers p_1, \dots, p_k is defined as (\cdots ((p_1 \oplus p_2) \oplus p_3) \oplus \cdots \oplus p_k), and it can be proved that this is independent of the order of p_1, \dots, p_k.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N,K \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 100
- 0 \leq A_i < 2^{20}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2 2
1 2 3

Sample Output 1

14

Here are the scores of the 2^3-1=7 non-empty subsequences of A.

- (1): 0
- (2): 0
- (3): 0
- (1,2): (1\oplus2)^2=9
- (1,3): (1\oplus3)^2=4
- (2,3): (2\oplus3)^2=1
- (1,2,3): 0

Therefore, the sought sum is 0+0+0+9+4+1+0=14.

Sample Input 2

10 5 3
100 100 100 100 100 100 100 100 100 100

Sample Output 2

252000000

Sample Input 3

16 4 100
7053 3876 3178 8422 7802 5998 2334 6757 6889 6637 7365 9495 7848 9026 7312 6558

Sample Output 3

432440016

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, one subtlety: The update relation (1-t)P_{a+1} = (1+t)P_a holds as polynomial identity exactly, and hence in R. Let's derive general solution carefully for implementation.

Given p length M, sum_p = 0 (mod MOD). Need p'.

Equations:
(1) p'_0 - p'_{M-1} = p_0 + p_{M-1}.
For r=1..M-1:
(2) p'_r - p'_{r-1} = p_r + p_{r-1}.

From (2) recursively:
p'_r = p'_0 + ∑_{j=1}^r (p_j + p_{j-1}) = p'_0 + u_r.
where u_r = ∑_{j=1}^r (p_j + p_{j-1}).

Then equation (1) requires p'_{M-1} = p'_0 - (p_0+p_{M-1}). But from formula p'_{M-1}=p'_0+u_{M-1}. Need u_{M-1}=-(p_0+p_{M-1}). This holds iff sum_p=0:
u_{M-1} = ∑_{j=1}^{M-1}(p_j+p_{j-1}) = (∑_{1}^{M-1}p_j) + (∑_{0}^{M-2}p_j) = (sum-p_0)+(sum-p_{M-1}) = 2sum - p_0 - p_{M-1}. If sum=0, u= -p0-p_{M-1}. Good.

Then U = ∑_{r=1}^{M-1} u_r. Sum p' = M p'_0 + U. target_sum = sum p'. So p'_0 = (target_sum - U)/M.

Need compute u_r efficiently. We can compute prefix sums. But if M=100, simple O(M) per update with inner loop computing u and U. 20M operations. Need optimize.

Now, FWHT of g modulo MOD: Need implement efficient. Let's write function:

def fwht_mod(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            for j in range(i, i+h):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j+h]
                v = x + y
                if v >= MOD: v -= MOD
                w = x - y
                if w < 0: w += MOD
                a[j] = v
                a[j+h] = w
        h = step

For exact:
def fwht_int(a):
    n=len(a); h=1
    while h<n:
        step=h<<1
        for i in range(0,n,step):
            for j in range(i,i+h):
                x=a[j]; y=a[j+h]
                a[j]=x+y
                a[j+h]=x-y
        h=step

Can we optimize by using local variables: MOD_local=MOD, a_local=a. In Python, local variable access faster. Use while loops instead of for? For loops over range are okay. But nested for with many range objects. Could use while j < i+h? Maybe slower. Let's keep for.

Potential issue: For g transform, x+y can be >=2MOD? x,y <MOD, so <2MOD, one subtract. x-y > -MOD, one add. Good.

Let's think about mathematical correctness for subsequences: A subsequence is determined by subset of indices, not by values. Our cnt counts elements by value; product over elements with multiplicity? The generating function ∏_{i=1}^N (1 + t X^{A_i}) counts each subset once. In Fourier transform, for each character s, F_s(t)=∏_i (1 + t χ_s(A_i)). Since χ_s(A_i) depends only on value, but if multiple equal values, factors repeated. We count n_s^+ and n_s^- by counts of values, so correct. Good.

Now, one subtlety: The score is (B_1 xor ... xor B_{|B|})^K, where B is a subsequence of A. If two different subsequences have same XOR and length, they contribute separately. Our count c_x counts subsequences, not distinct value combinations. The generating function product over indices counts each subset, so correct.

s=3 (011): dot 1=1,2=1,3=0? 3=011 dot 011 =0? popcount(3&3=3)=2 ->0, so -? Let's compute: 1&3=1 -> -, 2&3=2 -> -, 3&3=3 -> + => -1? -1-1+1=-1.
s=4 (100): dot all 0? 1&4=0,2&4=0,3&4=0 => 3.
s=5 (101): dot 1=1 (-),2=0 (+),3=1 (3&5=1) (-) => -1.
s=6 (110): dot 1=0 (+),2=1 (-),3=1 (3&6=2) (-) => -1.
s=7 (111): dot 1=1 (-),2=1 (-),3=0? 3&7=3 popcount2 -> + => -1.
So H: [3,-1,-1,-1,3,-1,-1,-1].

C for N=3,M=2: C[0]=4,C[1]=0,C[2]=0,C[3]=4. a=(3+H)/2:

Compute B_s = ∑_{x=0}^7 x^2 (-1)^{s·x}.
x^2: 0,1,4,9,16,25,36,49.
B_0 = sum =0+1+4+9+16+25+36+49=140.
B_4 (s=4): sign (-1)^{popcount(4&x)} = -1 for x with bit2=1 (4,5,6,7), + otherwise. Sum = (0+1+4+9) - (16+25+36+49) =14 -126 = -112.
Answer = inv8 *4*(140-112)= inv8 *4*28 = inv8*112=14. Good. Formula works.

Let's write C computation function:

def compute_C(N,M):
    pow2_N = pow(2,N,MOD)
    invM = pow(M, MOD-2, MOD)  # or precompute
    if M == 1:
        C = [0]*N + [pow2_N]  # length N+1
        return C
    d = M - 1
    # compute initial P0 vector
    p = [0]*M
    # comb initial
    inv = [0]*(N+1) maybe needed for P0. But if N large, we already need inv for comb. We can compute inv up to N. For recurrence, we also need invM. For C_init via vector update, need invM. We can compute inv array for binomial. But maybe we can compute P0 vector using factorial? Need inv. Let's keep inv array.
    # But if N < d, N small, still need inv up to N.
    # Compute p0 as before.
    # Then update p to get C_init.
    limit = d if N >= d else N+1  # number of C values needed before recurrence/done
    C = [0]*(N+1)
    for a in range(limit):
        C[a] = p[0]
        if a == N:  # only if N < limit? Actually if limit=N+1, after last no update.
            break
        target = pow2_N if a+1 == N else 0
        p = update(p, target, M, invM)
    if N < d:
        return C
    # Now C[0..d-1] filled. Need E_init.
    E = [C[i] * M % MOD for i in range(d)]
    # recurrence coefficients
    coeff_nonzero = []
    # compute comb M,k
    # small comb
    for k in range(3, M+1, 2):
        e = M - k
        c = comb(M,k) % MOD * invM % MOD
        coeff_nonzero.append((e, c))
    # If coeff_nonzero empty, recurrence E=0.
    window = E[:]
    for a in range(d, N+1):
        s = 0
        for idx, c in coeff_nonzero:
            s += c * window[idx]
        Ea = (-s) % MOD
        if a < N:
            C[a] = Ea * invM % MOD
        else:
            C[a] = (Ea + pow2_N) * invM % MOD
        window.pop(0)
        window.append(Ea)
    return C

def small_comb(n,k):
    if k<0 or k>n: return 0
    k=min(k,n-k)
    res=1
    for i in range(1,k+1):
        res = res*(n-k+i)//i
    return res

Let's write advance carefully with modular arithmetic.

def advance(p, target, M, invM):
    # p sum should be 0 unless? For updates from P_a to P_{a+1}, p=P_a. If a<N, sum=0. If a=N? not called.
    q = [0]*M  # But called few times, allocation okay.
    p0 = p[0]
    pref_prev = p0
    U = 0
    for r in range(1, M):
        pref_cur = pref_prev + p[r]
        if pref_cur >= MOD:
            pref_cur -= MOD
        u = pref_cur + pref_prev - p0
        if u < 0:
            u += MOD
        elif u >= MOD:
            u -= MOD
        U += u
        if U >= MOD:
            U -= MOD
        q[r] = u
        pref_prev = pref_cur
    p0_prime = (target - U) % MOD
    p0_prime = p0_prime * invM % MOD
    q[0] = p0_prime
    for r in range(1, M):
        v = q[r] + p0_prime
        if v >= MOD:
            v -= MOD
        q[r] = v
    return q

Need check U addition: U+u <2MOD, one subtract. U and u are [0,MOD). Good.

But what about pref_cur calculation: pref_prev + p[r] can be >=2MOD? pref_prev <MOD, p[r] <MOD, sum <2MOD, one subtract enough. Good.

u = pref_cur + pref_prev - p0. pref_cur+pref_prev <2MOD, minus p0 > -MOD, so <2MOD and > -MOD. One adjust enough. Good.

target - U: target in [0,MOD), U in [0,MOD), difference > -MOD, <MOD. `% MOD` okay. Could do if target < U: target += MOD; then subtract. But `%` fine for few calls.

Now, for recurrence scalar loop, we need C array. Let's implement compute_C:

def compute_C(N, M):
    pow2_N = pow(2, N, MOD)
    invM = pow(M, MOD-2, MOD)
    if M == 1:
        C = [0]*(N+1)
        C[N] = pow2_N
        return C
    # precompute inv up to N for binomial
    inv = [0]*(N+1)
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
        for i in range(2, N+1):
            inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
    p = [0]*M
    comb = 1
    for r in range(N+1):
        idx = r % M
        if r & 1:
            p[idx] -= comb
            if p[idx] < 0:
                p[idx] += MOD
        else:
            p[idx] += comb
            if p[idx] >= MOD:
                p[idx] -= MOD
        if r < N:
            comb = comb * (N - r) % MOD * inv[r+1] % MOD
    d = M - 1
    C = [0]*(N+1)
    limit = d if N >= d else N+1
    for a in range(limit):
        C[a] = p[0]
        if a == N:
            break
        target = pow2_N if a + 1 == N else 0
        p = advance(p, target, M, invM)
    if N < d:
        return C
    # recurrence
    E = [C[i] * M % MOD for i in range(d)]
    # nonzero coefficients
    coeff = []
    # compute comb(M,k) for odd k 3..M
    # small comb function inline
    for k in range(3, M+1, 2):
        # comb M choose k
        c = 1
        kk = k
        if kk > M - kk:
            kk = M - kk
        for i in range(1, kk+1):
            c = c * (M - kk + i) // i  # Wait formula for C(M, kk) if kk=min(k,M-k). Need careful.

For exact FWHT:
def fwht_int(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            j = i
            while j < end:
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j + h] = x - y
                j += 1
        h = step
Using while inner might be faster or slower than for? For loop over range is in C for iteration but still Python body. While loop avoids range object? It has manual j increment. Usually for loop is faster than while in Python because iteration in C. Use for j in range(i, i+h). Let's use for.

For mod FWHT:
def fwht_mod(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    mod = MOD
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            for j in range(i, i+h):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j+h]
                v = x + y
                if v >= mod:
                    v -= mod
                w = x - y
                if w < 0:
                    w += mod
                a[j] = v
                a[j+h] = w
        h = step

Could optimize by binding a local variable `arr=a`; already local. `mod=MOD` local. Good.

Potential issue: In mod FWHT, x+y can be >=2MOD? x,y <MOD, so max 2MOD-2, one subtract. x-y min -(MOD-1), one add. Good.

cnt = [0] * L
for val in A:

ans = ans * invL % MOD
print(ans)

Roots r_j = (1+ω^j)/(1-ω^j). We want polynomial with roots r_j. For each j, r_j satisfies (r-1)/(r+1) = ω^j? Let's solve:
r = (1+ω)/(1-ω)
r(1-ω)=1+ω => r-1 = ω(r+1) => ω = (r-1)/(r+1). Yes.
Thus r_j are roots of ((r-1)/(r+1))^M = 1, but j=0 gives r? For ω=1, equation r-1 = r+1 => -1=1 impossible, so r=∞. The finite roots for j=1..M-1 satisfy (r-1)^M = (r+1)^M. The polynomial Q(r)=(r-1)^M - (r+1)^M has degree M-1 and roots r_j. Leading coefficient: Expand (r-1)^M: leading r^M - M r^{M-1}+...; (r+1)^M: r^M + M r^{M-1}+...; difference = -2M r^{M-1}+ lower. So monic P(r)=Q(r)/(-2M). Now Q(r) = ∑_{k=0}^M C(M,k) r^{M-k} [(-1)^k - 1]. For k=0, 1-1=0. For k even, (-1)^k-1=0. For k odd, -2. So Q = -2∑_{k odd} C(M,k) r^{M-k}. Divide by -2M: P = ∑_{k odd} (C(M,k)/M) r^{M-k}. For k=1, coefficient C(M,1)/M =1, giving r^{M-1}. For k odd≥3, coefficient as used. Good. So recurrence P(r)=0 => r^d + ∑_{k odd≥3} C(M,k)/M r^{M-k}=0. Yes.

Let's write code carefully.

Pseudo code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def fwht_int(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            for j in range(i, end):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[j + h] = x - y
        h = step

def fwht_mod(a):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            for j in range(i, end):
                x = a[j]
                y = a[j + h]
                v = x + y
                if v >= mod:
                    v -= mod
                w = x - y
                if w < 0:
                    w += mod
                a[j] = v
                a[j + h] = w
        h = step

def advance(p, target, M, invM):
    mod = MOD
    q = [0] * M
    p0 = p[0]
    pref_prev = p0
    U = 0
    for r in range(1, M):
        pref_cur = pref_prev + p[r]
        if pref_cur >= mod:
            pref_cur -= mod
        u = pref_cur + pref_prev - p0
        if u < 0:
            u += mod
        elif u >= mod:
            u -= mod
        U += u
        if U >= mod:
            U -= mod
        q[r] = u
        pref_prev = pref_cur
    p0_prime = (target - U) % mod
    p0_prime = p0_prime * invM % mod
    q[0] = p0_prime
    for r in range(1, M):
        v = q[r] + p0_prime
        if v >= mod:
            v -= mod
        q[r] = v
    return q

def compute_C(N, M):
    mod = MOD
    pow2_N = pow(2, N, mod)
    invM = pow(M, mod-2, mod)
    if M == 1:
        C = [0] * (N + 1)
        C[N] = pow2_N
        return C
    # inv array
    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
        for i in range(2, N + 1):
            inv[i] = mod - (mod // i) * inv[mod % i] % mod
    p = [0] * M
    comb = 1
    for r in range(N + 1):
        idx = r % M
        if r & 1:
            p[idx] -= comb
            if p[idx] < 0:
                p[idx] += mod
        else:
            p[idx] += comb
            if p[idx] >= mod:
                p[idx] -= mod
        if r < N:
            comb = comb * (N - r) % mod * inv[r + 1] % mod
    # no longer need inv
    d = M - 1
    C = [0] * (N + 1)
    limit = d if N >= d else N + 1
    for a in range(limit):
        C[a] = p[0]
        if a == N:
            break
        target = pow2_N if a + 1 == N else 0
        p = advance(p, target, M, invM)
    if N < d:
        return C
    E = [C[i] * M % mod for i in range(d)]
    # combM
    combM = [0] * (M + 1)
    combM[0] = 1
    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        combM[i] = combM[i-1] * (M - i + 1) // i
    coeff = []
    for k in range(3, M + 1, 2):
        e = M - k
        c = (combM[k] % mod) * invM % mod
        coeff.append((e, c))
    window = E[:]
    for a in range(d, N + 1):
        s = 0
        for idx, c in coeff:
            s += c * window[idx]
        Ea = (-s) % mod
        if a < N:
            C[a] = Ea * invM % mod
        else:
            C[a] = (Ea + pow2_N) * invM % mod
        window.pop(0)
        window.append(Ea)
    return C

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N, M, K = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    A = data[3:]
    max_a = max(A)
    L = 1 << max_a.bit_length()
    cnt = [0] * L
    for a in A:
        cnt[a] += 1
    # free A, data maybe
    # compute C
    C = compute_C(N, M)
    fwht_int(cnt)
    g = [pow(i, K, MOD) for i in range(L)]
    fwht_mod(g)
    invL = pow(L, MOD-2, MOD)
    ans = 0
    # local vars
    C_local = C
    cnt_local = cnt
    g_local = g
    N_local = N
    for s in range(L):
        h = cnt_local[s]
        a = (N_local + h) >> 1
        ans += C_local[a] * g_local[s]
    ans %= MOD
    ans = ans * invL % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353


def fwht_int(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            for j in range(i, end):
                k = j + h
                x = a[j]
                y = a[k]
                a[j] = x + y
                a[k] = x - y
        h = step


def fwht_mod(a):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)
    h = 1
    while h < n:
        step = h << 1
        for i in range(0, n, step):
            end = i + h
            for j in range(i, end):
                k = j + h
                x = a[j]
                y = a[k]
                v = x + y
                if v >= mod:
                    v -= mod
                w = x - y
                if w < 0:
                    w += mod
                a[j] = v
                a[k] = w
        h = step


def advance(p, target, m, inv_m):
    mod = MOD
    q = [0] * m
    p0 = p[0]
    pref_prev = p0
    u_sum = 0

    for r in range(1, m):
        pref_cur = pref_prev + p[r]
        if pref_cur >= mod:
            pref_cur -= mod

        u = pref_cur + pref_prev - p0
        if u < 0:
            u += mod
        elif u >= mod:
            u -= mod

        u_sum += u
        if u_sum >= mod:
            u_sum -= mod

        q[r] = u
        pref_prev = pref_cur

    p0_prime = (target - u_sum) % mod
    p0_prime = p0_prime * inv_m % mod
    q[0] = p0_prime

    for r in range(1, m):
        v = q[r] + p0_prime
        if v >= mod:
            v -= mod
        q[r] = v

    return q


def compute_C(N, M):
    mod = MOD
    pow2_N = pow(2, N, mod)
    inv_m = pow(M, mod - 2, mod)

    if M == 1:
        C = [0] * (N + 1)
        C[N] = pow2_N
        return C

    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
        for i in range(2, N + 1):
            inv[i] = mod - (mod // i) * inv[mod % i] % mod

    # P_0(t) = (1 - t)^N modulo (t^M - 1)
    p = [0] * M
    comb = 1
    for r in range(N + 1):
        idx = r % M
        if r & 1:
            p[idx] -= comb
            if p[idx] < 0:
                p[idx] += mod
        else:
            p[idx] += comb
            if p[idx] >= mod:
                p[idx] -= mod
        if r < N:
            comb = comb * (N - r) % mod * inv[r + 1] % mod

    d = M - 1
    C = [0] * (N + 1)

    limit = d if N >= d else N + 1
    for a in range(limit):
        C[a] = p[0]
        if a + 1 < limit:
            target = pow2_N if a + 1 == N else 0
            p = advance(p, target, M, inv_m)

    if N < d:
        return C

    # E_a = sum_{j=1}^{M-1} (1-omega^j)^N * r_j^a
    E = [C[i] * M % mod for i in range(d)]

    # Characteristic polynomial:
    # P(x) = x^(M-1) + sum_{k odd>=3} C(M,k)/M * x^(M-k)
    combM = [0] * (M + 1)
    combM[0] = 1
    for i in range(1, M + 1):
        combM[i] = combM[i - 1] * (M - i + 1) // i

    coeff = []
    for k in range(3, M + 1, 2):
        e = M - k
        c = (combM[k] % mod) * inv_m % mod
        coeff.append((e, c))

    window = E[:]
    for a in range(d, N + 1):
        s = 0
        for idx, c in coeff:
            s += c * window[idx]
        Ea = (-s) % mod

        if a < N:
            C[a] = Ea * inv_m % mod
        else:
            C[a] = (Ea + pow2_N) * inv_m % mod

        window.pop(0)
        window.append(Ea)

    return C


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M, K = data[0], data[1], data[2]
    A = data[3:]

    max_a = max(A)
    L = 1 << max_a.bit_length()

    cnt = [0] * L
    for a in A:
        cnt[a] += 1

    del data, A

    C = compute_C(N, M)

    # H_s = sum_i (-1)^{popcount(s & A_i)}
    fwht_int(cnt)

    # B_s = sum_x x^K (-1)^{popcount(s & x)}
    g = [pow(i, K, MOD) for i in range(L)]
    fwht_mod(g)

    invL = pow(L, MOD - 2, MOD)

    ans = 0
    C_local = C
    cnt_local = cnt
    g_local = g
    N_local = N

    for s in range(L):
        h = cnt_local[s]
        a = (N_local + h) >> 1
        ans += C_local[a] * g_local[s]

    ans %= MOD
    ans = ans * invL % MOD
    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
